The raw mechanism contrast is OUT_SL = +1.14 [0.60, 1.56] log-odds for Gemma, and the input-language contrast is IN_SL = -0.60 [-1.02, -0.18]. For GaMS3, the input-language contrast is IN_SL = -0.86 [-1.58, -0.13], which excludes zero.\\n\\n[FIGURE:fig_grid]\\n\\n**Table 2: Refusal rates at high edit dose, Gemma (harmful items, 200 per cell). Cells with <90% language compliance are marked with \\\\dag.**\\n\\n| | EN out | SL out | HU out |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| EN in | 0.18 | 0.77 | 0.46 |\\n| SL in | 0.07 | 0.56 | 0.53 |\\n| HU in | 0.10 | 0.83 | 0.55 |\\n\\n**Table 3: Refusal rates at high edit dose, GaMS3 (harmful items, 200 per cell). Cells with <90% language compliance are marked with \\\\dag.**\\n\\n| | EN out | SL out | HU out |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| EN in | 0.21 | 0.14 | 0.23 |\\n| SL in | 0.16\\\\dag | 0.15 | 0.17\\\\dag |\\n| HU in | 0.19\\\\dag | 0.12\\\\dag | 0.28 |\\n\\nThis finding is rated ESTIMATE for three reasons. First, the PPI-corrected output-language contrast for Gemma is 0.20 [-0.76, 1.13], consistent with zero. Second, the pre-registered four-readout robustness gate yields robust = False for the Slovene output-language call. Third, the adjudicator achieves kappa = 0.12 with the primary judge in the Gemma Slovene-edited cell (Se = 0.927, Sp = 0.714, n = 48), indicating that the judge-specificity anchor is weak. Experiment 16 provides a confirmatory point estimate (OUT_Gemma = +2.43 [+0.89, +5.38] on a held-out 294-item set), but the judge-specificity concern persists.\\n\\n**Compliant-row contrast.** Restricting to rows where both models achieve >90% compliance (EN-input rows only), the edit-induced refusal lag in the EN->SL cell is -1.26 [-2.17, 0.20], which includes zero.\\n\\n### 4.3 Cross-Model Specificity\\n\\nThe cross-model contrasts test whether Gemma's pattern differs from GaMS3's. The output-language specificity contrast is dOUT_SL = -1.58 [-2.22, -0.66] (MDE 1.07), rated DIFFERENT: Gemma's output-language effect is larger than GaMS3's. The input-language specificity contrast is dIN_SL = -0.26 [-1.13, 0.57] (MDE 1.19), rated ESTIMATE: the two models do not measurably differ in input-language sensitivity.\\n\\n### 4.4 Refusal Composition\\n\\nAmong Gemma's Slovene refusals at high dose (eval4 adjudication, n = 30 per cell), the rate of any refusal is 0.20 [0.10, 0.37] for GaMS3 EN-output versus 0.10 [0.03, 0.26] for GaMS3 SL-output. Of Gemma's Slovene refusals, 54% are explicit (the model states that it cannot help) and 26% are deflections (the model redirects the conversation without directly refusing). [ARTIFACT:gen_art_evaluation_4]\\n\\n### 4.5 Dose-Response\\n\\n[FIGURE:fig_dose]\\n\\nExperiment 9 measures refusal rates across nine lambda values for both models in English and Slovene (300 items per condition). Gemma's dose-response curve in Slovene is right-shifted relative to its English curve: at matched doses, more Slovene refusal remains. GaMS3 shows a smaller and non-significant shift. The 13-step dense dose ladder (Experiment 15) confirms that the refusal gap is present across the dose range, not concentrated at a single operating point. [ARTIFACT:gen_art_experiment_9]\\n\\n### 4.6 Inconclusive and Null Results\\n\\n**G3_orig (unedited GaMS3 vs Gemma in SL->SL).** The unedited GaMS3 shows a refusal gap of -1.17 [-2.83, 0.22] relative to Gemma in the SL->SL cell. This estimate is inconclusive: it includes zero, its fragility index is 1 (a single item reclassification would flip the sign), and the unedited models operate near ceiling refusal rates (Gemma 0.95, GaMS3 0.87), making log-odds contrasts unreliable.\\n\\n**Hungarian.** All Hungarian contrasts are uninformative. The minimum detectable effect for Hungarian is approximately 3 log-odds units, and no observed Hungarian effect exceeds this threshold.\\n\\n**RefusEU validation.** Experiment 17 tests ASR on the reserved RefusEU benchmark (1,300 EN + 1,300 SL items). The DiD on this benchmark is inconclusive (MDE > 2m), consistent with the near-ceiling refusal rates on this corpus. [ARTIFACT:gen_art_experiment_17]\\n\\n## 5 Discussion\\n\\n### 5.1 Interpretation\\n\\nThe over-refusal finding has a concrete interpretation for multilingual evaluation: a model can appear \\\"safer\\\" in a non-English language simply because it refuses more benign requests, inflating the refusal rate without any improvement in harm detection. The signal-detection framing separates these two components. The near-zero DiD on d' means that the language shift does not make either model better or worse at distinguishing harmful from benign items; the entire difference is in the threshold.\\n\\nThe reply-language finding, if confirmed by future work with stronger judge anchors, would suggest that the refusal mechanism is partially encoded in the model's output-language circuitry rather than the input-language pathway. This is consistent with mechanistic findings that safety-related representations are entangled with language-identity features \\\\cite{Upadhyaya2026, Miao2026}.\\n\\n### 5.2 Limitations\\n\\n**Sample size.** The adjudication sample is 30 items per cell, which limits the precision of judge-validity estimates. The false-alarm rate estimates rest on 1,093 safe items, adequate for the pooled contrast but noisy at the per-category level.\\n\\n**Judge specificity.** The primary judge (distilled mdeBERTa) achieves Sp = 0.714 in the Gemma Slovene-edited cell, meaning roughly 29% of non-refusals are misclassified as refusals. This inflates the raw refusal rate and weakens the reply-language finding. The PPI correction addresses this statistically but depends on the gold-label quality of the Sonnet 4.5 adjudicator, which is itself an LLM.\\n\\n**Language compliance.** Four cells (SL->EN, HU->EN, SL->HU, HU->SL) fall below 90% compliance, limiting the crossed-grid analysis to compliant rows for mechanism claims.\\n\\n**Quantization.** Both models run in NF4 quantization, which introduces noise relative to full-precision inference. The abliteration edit is applied to the quantized model, and the interaction between quantization and directional ablation is not characterized.\\n\\n**Novelty.** The observation that over-refusal rates differ across languages is partially anticipated by Addagada \\\\cite{Addagada2026} and Nguyen \\\\cite{Nguyen2026}. The present work's contribution is the signal-detection decomposition (criterion vs. sensitivity) and the controlled abliteration design, not the observation itself.\\n\\n## 6 Conclusion\\n\\nUnedited Gemma-3-12B-IT over-refuses benign Slovene prompts at a rate of 0.569, compared to 0.364 in English. Signal-detection analysis attributes this entirely to a criterion shift (DiD_c = +0.52 [0.38, 0.68]) with no sensitivity difference, replicated across four experiments. A secondary analysis suggests that residual refusal under partial abliteration tracks the reply language, but this finding is rated ESTIMATE due to weak judge anchoring (kappa = 0.12) and a PPI correction consistent with zero. Multilingual safety evaluations that report only attack-success rates on harmful items will miss criterion shifts that inflate refusal rates on benign items.\\n\\n\\\\bibliography{references}\\n\\\\bibliographystyle{plainnat}\",\n  \"figures\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"fig_overview\",\n      \"figure_type\": \"concept\",\n      \"title\": \"Study Design Overview\",\n      \"caption\": \"Overview of the experimental design. The residual refusal is measured on safe and harmful items across languages and decomposed into criterion (threshold) and sensitivity (discriminability) components using signal-detection theory.\",\n      \"image_gen_detailed_description\": \"A concept diagram showing the experimental pipeline. # Over-Refusal of Benign Slovene Prompts Survives English De-censoring in Gemma-3-12B

````
This repository contains the paper draft and supporting materials for a study of multilingual over-refusal behavior under partial abliteration edits.

## Key Finding

Gemma-3-12B-IT false-refuses benign Slovene items at nearly twice the English rate (0.569 vs 0.364). Signal-detection analysis attributes this entirely to a criterion shift (DiD_c = +0.52 [0.38, 0.68]) with no change in sensitivity, replicated across four experiments. The Slovene-adapted sibling GaMS3-12B-Instruct shows no such language-dependent criterion shift. A secondary reply-language refusal effect is rated ESTIMATE due to weak judge anchoring (kappa = 0.12) and a PPI correction consistent with zero.

## Repository Layout

```
.
├── paper_draft.md                        # Full paper text with [FIGURE:] and [ARTIFACT:] markers
├── .terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json # Structured output (title, abstract, paper_text, figures, summary)
├── references.bib                        # BibTeX bibliography (27 entries via Semantic Scholar / OpenAlex)
├── references.json                       # Fetch provenance records for each bibliography entry
├── README.md                             # This file
└── .aii/
    └── manifest.yaml                     # Artifact manifest for the AI Inventor pipeline
```

## How to Use

The primary output is `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`, which contains:

- **title** and **abstract** as standalone fields
- **paper_text** in Markdown with `[FIGURE:fig_id]` markers where figures go and `[ARTIFACT:artifact_id]` markers linking claims to experiment code
- **figures** array with `id`, `figure_type`, `title`, `caption`, `image_gen_detailed_description`, and `summary` for each figure
- **summary** of the paper

The downstream pipeline reads this JSON to generate figure images from the `image_gen_detailed_description` fields and to compile the final paper.

`paper_draft.md` is a human-readable copy of the same paper text.

## Models

- **Gemma-3-12B-IT** (`google/gemma-3-12b-it`, rev 96b6f1ec)
- **GaMS3-12B-Instruct** (`cjvt/GaMS3-12B-Instruct`, rev 1d0b27af)

Both run in NF4 quantization. Abliteration via per-layer directional ablation with LoRA adapters, optimized by 40-trial TPE.

## Artifacts Referenced

| Artifact | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| gen_art_experiment_5 | Criterion shift on safe items (XSTest/OR-Bench, 1,093 safe + 349 harmful items) |
| gen_art_experiment_9 | Lambda dose-response curves (300 items, 9 steps) |
| gen_art_experiment_11 | Hungarian control language confirmation |
| gen_art_experiment_12 | Capability damage check (6 benchmarks, 300 paired items) |
| gen_art_experiment_14 | Crossed grid (3x3 languages, 200 items, mechanism contrasts) |
| gen_art_experiment_15 | Dense 13-step dose ladder |
| gen_art_experiment_16 | Held-out confirmation (294 items, PPI estimator) |
| gen_art_experiment_17 | ASR on reserved RefusEU (1,300 EN + 1,300 SL) |
| gen_art_evaluation_4 | Error matrices and adjudication (Sonnet 4.5, n=30 per cell) |
````
